**Structure:** Escalating elimination-tournament format, licensed off the fictional TV IP Squid Game: six sequential eliminating games (red light/green light, honeycomb carving, tug of war, marbles, ddakji, glass bridge, musical chairs) narrow 456 real contestants down to one winner, with running eliminated-count updates ("232 of you survived," "142 of you survived honeycomb") reinforcing stakes at every stage.
**Framing:** Real-world, real-money recreation of a globally recognized fictional format: borrowed IP recognition plus a concrete, escalating cash number as the throughline; mid-video sponsor integration (Brawl Stars) staged as an in-game elimination mechanic rather than a break.

**Structure:** Escalating price-tier comparison format applied to boats: $1 sinking rowboat, $1M, $10M, $25M, $50M (celebrity cameo from Pete Davidson), $300M (celebrity cameo from Tom Brady), up to a $1B "floating city" cruise ship with its own neighborhoods, waterpark, and mall; each tier's room count and amenities are explicitly tallied against the tier before it.
**Framing:** First-person "tour every price tier" format identical in mechanic to the plane and car videos but applied to yachts; two celebrity-guest segments (Pete Davidson name-drop, an extended Tom Brady catch/football scene) function as social proof for the top tiers; heavy recurring Feastables product integration throughout, plus a subscriber giveaway (free three-night cruise) and a paid-purchase CTA (buy Feastables to unlock a "secret" unreleased video) at the close.

**Structure:** Single-contestant, 45-day confinement-for-cash format: one person (Alex) lives inside a bought-out grocery store and earns $10,000 per day, but must "sell back" $10,000 worth of store product each day to keep earning, forcing continuous resource-management strategy; a mid-video power-cutoff twist (frozen goods spoiling, no lights) escalates difficulty, and a family-visit reward beat provides an emotional turn before the final decision to cash out.
**Framing:** Long-duration single-subject survival documentary style narrated in voiceover past tense ("On day five, he sold me...") rather than pure real-time footage, building a full character arc for the contestant (building a shower, a wall, a pool) rather than a multi-person competition; two sponsor segments (Shopify, Kanga Coolers) integrated as in-story problem-solving tools; ends on a suspense-fake ("bring it back in... just kidding") before revealing the final payout.

**Structure:** Seven-day real-time survival vlog format with a small fixed cast (Jimmy/MrBeast plus four friends including a recurring "cursed" character, Mack, framed around his prior money-losing appearances); day-by-day chaptering (shelter building, chicken trapping, fishing, a ruins expedition, a storm night) rather than an elimination or prize-tournament structure, with a mid-video twist offering Mack a scripted "third chance" redemption arc.
**Framing:** Slice-of-life ensemble survival documentary, narrated partly in past-tense recap ("day two is coming to an end... how would you say it was") between real-time scenes, leaning on interpersonal banter and running jokes (the chicken named Bryan, Mack's losing streak) as much as physical hardship; two sponsor integrations (Feastables s'mores, a Western Union-funded supply crate) woven directly into the survival narrative; closes on a plain thank-you to viewers rather than a giveaway or subscribe gimmick.

**Framing:** Large-scale demographic-spectacle format using age as the sole identifying label and organizing lens for contestants, generating narrative through generational alliances/rivalries and a running "graveyard of Xs" visual tracking eliminations; a Shop app sponsor segment is woven into a mid-game elimination round; ends on an emotional family-surprise beat for a finalist and a consolation prize for the runner-up, plus a subscribe-to-win CTA.

**Structure:** Escalating price-tier travel/lodging comparison spanning seven stays ($1 floor mat, $1,000 cave, $10,000 underwater villa, $50,000 private island vs. a $50 bus side-quest, a $75,000 one-hour chartered cruise ship cash hunt, $150,000 Vegas suites, and a $1M castle), each demonstrated by actually sleeping there and touring its specific amenities rather than just listing them.
**Framing:** Multi-location travelogue "we tried every price tier" format filmed across real-world international locations rather than a built set, with a parallel storyline (two crew members stuck in a $50 bus) used as a comedic low-end foil against the escalating luxury; two sponsor integrations (Experian, folded into a cash-hunt challenge on the chartered cruise ship) and a subscriber-guest segment at the top tier; closes on a drone-and-fireworks finale and a subscribe CTA tied to being cast in a future video.

**Structure:** 100-day two-stranger isolation-endurance format (both must survive the full term together to win, or both get nothing): the narrative is chaptered by periodic "day X offer" temptations every ten days (a private chef, comfortable beds, a mystery box, a family visit, a blind-bid "everything" auction), each testing whether the pair will sacrifice prize money for comfort; ends on a Golden-Balls-style split-or-steal finale.
**Framing:** Long-duration two-person psychological/relationship documentary rather than a multi-contestant tournament, tracking the arc from strangers to friction to camaraderie under confinement; a running "what we overheard them wish for" mechanic drives each surprise offer, creating dramatic irony for the viewer; one sponsor integration (Shop app) woven into the climactic auction sequence; ends on a trust-game climax (split or steal) rather than a simple survive/leave outcome.

**Structure:** Seven-day real-time raft-survival vlog with a small friend cast (including creator guest LazarBeam), chaptered day-by-day around building and repeatedly rebuilding a shelter, catching (failing to catch) fish, rationing food and water, and enduring a multi-day storm that destroys their shelter; a recurring wild-animal mascot (a bird named Bryan) provides a through-line comic/emotional beat.
**Framing:** Unscripted-feeling ensemble endurance documentary leaning hard into visible physical misery (seasickness, contaminated water, storm damage, sunburn, jellyfish sting) as the core content rather than competition or prize tension; explicit callbacks to the channel's own prior extreme-endurance videos (burying himself alive, solitary confinement) frame this as harder than those; one sponsor integration (Shopify) woven into storm-prep footage; ends on a simple relief-at-rescue beat and a sponsor-tied freebie CTA rather than a subscribe push.

**Structure:** Escalating price-tier travel comparison across seven islands ($1 bare islet, $1M monkey-populated island, $5M frozen/hovercraft island, $16M submarine-and-skydiving island, $45M ruins island, $150M Richard Branson-owned exotic-animal island, $250M waterpark island), each tier's defining feature (monkeys, ice hovercrafts, submarines, ancient ruins, endangered wildlife, a 135-foot waterslide) driving that segment.
**Framing:** International travelogue "we tried every price tier" format identical in mechanic to the plane/car/yacht/house entries in this ladder series, distinguished here by real celebrity ownership (Richard Branson's island) and heavy use of exotic/endangered animals as luxury signifiers; one sponsor integration (Stumble Guys mobile game) staged as a literal shark-cage dare mid-video; ends on the tallest-waterslide-in-North-America stunt as the closing set piece rather than a giveaway or subscribe CTA.

**Structure:** Six-fear escalating gauntlet for recurring contestant Mack (a "cursed loser" from earlier videos, callback to the Deadliest Obstacle Course entry's protagonist), each stage a distinct staged fear (a spider-filled box, an unclipped 200-foot freefall, a sinking car with cash-filled duffel bags, an unknown object reach-in box, being buried alive for an undisclosed duration, and a 1:1 recreation of the umbrella-cookie challenge he previously lost), with money escalating or being lost at each stage rather than a flat prize.
**Framing:** Single-hero psychological-endurance format built on the meta-premise that the contestant claims immunity to fear, using his own dismissiveness ("you cannot scare me") as narrative tension the video sets out to disprove; explicit callback structure to his prior on-channel loss (the same umbrella-cookie set rebuilt as the finale); two sponsor integrations (a Feastables rebrand reveal disguised as a scare box, Shopify woven into the reveal of the burial's actual duration) embedded directly in challenge mechanics; transcript closes on an ambiguous note before a clear final-outcome line, consistent with the channel's habit of building suspense right up to (and sometimes past) the visible transcript.

**Structure:** Full-set movie-recreation elimination format (a built-to-scale Willy Wonka's Chocolate Factory) with a sequence of themed mini-games matching the film's iconography (hide and seek, a candy rock-climbing wall, a competitive-eating round with guest speed-eaters Matt Stonie and Joey Chestnut, a Mentos-in-giant-Coke-bottle shot, a spinning-carousel balance game, a fake-cake-toilet lottery, and a Gordon Ramsay-judged cooking finale) narrowing ten Feastables golden-ticket winners down to one factory owner.
**Framing:** Licensed-IP theme-park recreation format (echoing the Squid Game entry's mechanic) built entirely around a real product tie-in: contestants qualified by finding a golden ticket in a Feastables bar, with two celebrity-judge cameos (competitive eaters, Gordon Ramsay) supplying expert legitimacy at different stages; closes on an epilogue twist where MrBeast buys the "won" factory back from the winner for cash, undercutting the prize with a comic reversal instead of a straight subscribe CTA.

**Structure:** Single mystery-button anthology format: friends take turns pressing one button that dispenses a random outcome from a long, escalating list (gross-out dares, physical endurance tasks, waiting games, generous side-giveaways to strangers), with exactly one of the many presses secretly worth $100,000 for the presser's sister; the "punishment" pool is large enough that the video runs to over 50 recorded presses before the prize hits.
**Framing:** Randomized-outcome anthology format (distinct from the escalating price-tier or elimination-tournament formats elsewhere on the channel) where suspense comes from not knowing which press is the real prize rather than a visible bracket or countdown; a self-imposed "skip" button (usable once per person) adds a strategic wrinkle; no sponsor integration; frames the win as a gift to a family member (the presser's sister) rather than personal winnings, and closes on a plain subscribe ask.

**Framing:** Single-contestant risk-escalation format built entirely around the "double or nothing" decision point repeated after every trap, generating tension from Mack's own greed/exhaustion rather than from other competitors; explicit self-referential callback to the channel's own Squid Game video as a direct trap inspiration; one sponsor-adjacent beat (a Feastables-vs-Hershey's "easy" trap); ends on a rare loss (Mack breaks the final cookie and forfeits everything) softened by a post-loss consolation payment, framed as a moral aside ("quit while you're ahead") rather than a triumphant win.

**Framing:** Pure physical/psychological-endurance solo vlog distinct from every prize-driven format elsewhere in this sample, generating tension purely from confinement (claustrophobia, bathroom logistics, boredom) and slapstick friend-group pranking rather than competition or money; addresses camera-skepticism directly ("people are always like, fake, you didn't show us how you used the bathroom"); no sponsor integration, one self-promotional merch aside; ends on unfiltered relief and a plain thank-you rather than a subscribe CTA or reveal.
